It controls epistemic status and containment, never evidence visibility or presumptive acceptance/rejection.","legacy_docket_assessment":{"adjudicable":false,"packet":null,"status":"invalid"},"packet_scope_relation":"exact_declared_pericope","reader_identity":[{"focus_ref":"95:2","identity_status":"legacy_unbound","packet_identity":null,"protocol":"focus-trace-hermetic-response-v4","reader_id":"reader_hft_a","trace_kind":"reconstructed"}]},"reader_synthesis_count":9,"source_present":true,"structured_insight_count":16,"unstructured_record_count":0},"identity":{"ayah_ref":"95:2","lane":"global","linguistic_source_ref":"95:2","surface_ref":"95:2","unit_kind":"numbered_ayah"},"primary_floor":{"source_ref":"95:2","target_tokens":[["Sina",["95:2:2"]],["Dağı'na",["95:2:1","95:2:2"]],["da",["95:2:1"]],["andolsun",["95:2:1","95:2:2"]]],"text":"Sina Dağı'na da andolsun."},"schema_version":"commentary-v5-hermetic-scope-packet-v1","scope":{"hft":{"assigned_record_count":9,"authoring_policy":{"every_assigned_record_requires_explicit_review":true,"parseable_hft_is_always_visible_to_authoring":true,"provenance_affects_qualification_not_visibility":true,"scope_mismatch_affects_lane_assignment_not_visibility":true,"unresolved_branch_citations_do_not_suppress_records":true},"authoring_status":"visible_with_provenance_qualification"},"lane_contract":"reader walks and cross-run publications with explicit trust labels","pericope":{"ayah_from":1,"ayah_to":8,"id":"s095-p01-001-008","label":"Whole surah","number":1,"refs":["95:1","95:2","95:3","95:4","95:5","95:6","95:7","95:8"]},"readiness":{"authoring_effect":"Docket readiness is reported for source provenance. HFT visibility is governed by the authoring policy above.","docket_mode":"quarantine_without_hft","docket_ready":true}},"selected_context_units":[],"support_registry":[{"branch_refs":["root_000955/B004"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:2","source_type":"v12_reader_walks","support_id":"sup_02ca3059f27bfcd07515","text":"2. **Stages and turns hidden inside the mountain word.** Lexical evidence: ط و ر B004, successive turns and states; خ ل ق B001/B003 in 95:4, proportioning and complete form; ر د د B001/B010 in 95:5, return and repetition/wavering. Structural cue: after طور comes a sequence: creation in best taqwim, then return to the lowest, then exception. Assigned role: طور activates \"phase\" as well as \"mountain,\" making the surah's later temporal movement visible in advance. Abductive move: I infer that the same root's stage-branch can be activated by the packet's following state-changes even when the surface is a mountain noun. Reading change: 95:2 becomes an anticipatory marker of phases in human becoming, not only a geographic oath.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000148/B001","root_000955/B001","root_000955/B002"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:3","source_type":"v12_reader_walks","support_id":"sup_1d4ebc12e66f28380b85","text":"3. **Approaching a precinct rather than merely naming a mountain.** Lexical evidence: ط و ر B001, extended perimeter/frontage; ط و ر B002, nearing the surrounding precinct; ب ل د B001/ء م ن B001 in 95:3, bounded secure land. Structural cue: \"طور\" is followed by \"this secure town\"; both are oath-sites with implied surrounding spaces. Assigned role: ط و ر functions as a perimeter one approaches, and البلد as the secured bounded tract inside the next oath unit. Abductive move: I supply the spatial movement from perimeter to protected city because the ayat move from mountain to town. Reading change: the ayah reads as approach to a protected sacred zone, not only as a named elevation.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000131/B002","root_000955/B004"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:3","source_type":"v12_reader_walks_wide","support_id":"sup_32d72f20db8479bf6368","text":"3. **Successive states latent in the Mount.** **Lexical evidence:** fixed `ط و ر/B004` supplies repeated turns and successive stages, including stages of creation. **Structural cue:** the window proceeds through creation in a best setting (95:4), “then” a downward return (95:5), an exception (95:6), and temporal “after” (95:7; `ب ع د/B002`). **Assigned role:** the stage branch turns *ṭūr* into a process-image that anticipates the sequence of human states. **Abductive move:** the packet supplies the stages and transition markers but does not call them *aṭwār*; I infer that their unusually explicit succession activates this dormant branch of the fixed root. **Reading change:** the oath can announce not just a place but a staged anthropology: formation, reversal, differentiated exception, and subsequent reckoning.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000148/B001","root_000955/B001"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:2","source_type":"v12_reader_walks_wide","support_id":"sup_3956de102820090189a3","text":"2. **From perimeter to protected interior.** **Lexical evidence:** fixed `ط و ر/B001` is an extended perimeter, `ط و ر/B002` approach to a surrounding precinct, and `ط و ر/B003` a boundary reached or crossed. **Structural cue:** the mount is immediately followed by the deictic “this bounded land, the secure/trustworthy” (95:3; `ب ل د/B001`, `ء م ن/B001`). **Assigned role:** the *ṭūr* branches supply edge, approach, and threshold; the next ayah supplies the bounded interior and its security. **Abductive move:** the packet does not explicitly describe motion; I infer a spatial progression perimeter → approach → protected jurisdiction from the lexical sequence. **Reading change:** the mount can function not only as an elevated object but as the threshold by which the oath enters ordered human space.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000955/B004"],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"finding-2","source_type":"cross_run_publication","support_id":"sup_3f65b3a047bbe90d1df7","text":"{\"anchors\":[[\"95:2:1\",\"root_000955\",[\"B004\"]]],\"grade\":\"strong\",\"text\":\"Sina Dağı, yaratılma, aşağıya döndürülme ve istisna dizisiyle birlikte insanın art arda geçtiği evreleri de önden bildirir.\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":["root_000190/B002","root_000955/B005"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:1","source_type":"v12_reader_walks_wide","support_id":"sup_5cd1d7df456925764e3a","text":"1. **A topographic hinge in the oath sequence.** **Lexical evidence:** fixed `ط و ر/B005` supplies the Mount or a mountain. **Structural cue:** it stands between the accepted mountain/place branch `ت ي ن/B002` in 95:1 and the bounded land `ب ل د/B001` in 95:3. **Assigned role:** *ṭūr* is the unmistakably elevated landmark that stabilizes a possible spatial reading on either side. **Abductive move:** the packet supplies adjacency but no route; I infer that the middle mountain organizes the three oath units as a traversable landscape. **Reading change:** the ayah reads as a geographic hinge linking cultivated place to secure settlement, while retaining its direct reference to a mountain.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000955/B001","root_000955/B002","root_000955/B003","root_000955/B005"],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"finding-1","source_type":"cross_run_publication","support_id":"sup_7620a1695306d4a731ab","text":"{\"anchors\":[[\"95:2:1\",\"root_000955\",[\"B001\",\"B002\",\"B003\",\"B005\"]]],\"grade\":\"strong\",\"text\":\"Sina Dağı, incir-zeytin arazisi ile güvenli şehir arasında çevreden içeriye yaklaşmayı ve korunan alana geçişi düzenleyen bir eşik olur.\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":["root_000059/B001","root_000955/B006"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:4","source_type":"v12_reader_walks_wide","support_id":"sup_85009fbedc91e31558a0","text":"4. **A limit between wild exterior and human order.** **Lexical evidence:** fixed `ط و ر/B006` supplies wildness beyond familiar human company. Later `ء ن س/B001` defines visible human presence over against the wild, and 95:3 supplies a secure city. **Structural cue:** the sequence moves from mount to city and then explicitly to *al-insān* (95:4). **Assigned role:** the wild branch marks the exterior side of a boundary; city and human presence mark the social side. **Abductive move:** the packet supplies the lexical opposition but not a theory of settlement; I infer that the mount occupies the liminal edge from which accountable human order comes into view. **Reading change:** *ṭūr* gains an anthropological boundary function in addition to its topographic one.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000955/B003","root_000955/B004"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:retrospective_surprises_md:1","source_type":"v12_reader_walks","support_id":"sup_a83ae5aae818edc48b30","text":"The later ثم رددناه in 95:5 most strongly activated ط و ر B004, \"successive turns and states\"; without 95:5, the mountain reading might have dominated. 95:7-8 also made ط و ر B003, boundary limit, more important: denial after evidence is a crossing or failure at the limit, and final judgment decides it. The surprise is that the mountain becomes a phase-marker and boundary-marker at once.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000955/B006","root_000955/B007"],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"finding-4","source_type":"cross_run_publication","support_id":"sup_a98a2f12b039df0fc093","text":"{\"anchors\":[[\"95:2:1\",\"root_000955\",[\"B006\",\"B007\"]]],\"grade\":\"weak\",\"text\":\"Dağın yabani ve insansız dış alan anlamları, güvenli şehir ve insanın tanıdık toplumsal varlığı karşısında medeni düzenin sınırını belirler.\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":["root_000955/B003"],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"finding-3","source_type":"cross_run_publication","support_id":"sup_af6c916b2e392ac08812","text":"{\"anchors\":[[\"95:2:1\",\"root_000955\",[\"B003\"]]],\"grade\":\"weak\",\"text\":\"Sınır anlamı, sonraki inkâr sorusuyla ahlaki bir eşiğe dönüşür: delillerden sonra inkâr, uygun sınırı aşma gibi görünür.\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":["root_000955/B003","root_000955/B005"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:activated_readings_md:1","source_type":"v12_reader_walks","support_id":"sup_c475464400cba30b424f","text":"1. **The Mount as a boundary marker before crossing into human valuation.** Lexical evidence: ط و ر B005, the Mount/mountain; ط و ر B003, boundary limit reached or exceeded. Structural cue: 95:2 stands between the fruit/place pair of 95:1 and the secure town of 95:3, immediately before the created human in 95:4. Assigned role: طور supplies a height/place, while its boundary branch marks a threshold between witnessed signs and the claim about the human. Abductive move: I supply the arrow from spatial mountain to evaluative limit: the packet uses topography to stage the question of whether the human remains within proper bounds. Reading change: the Mount reads less as a static landmark and more as a limit-post in the surah's moral geometry.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"branch_refs":["root_000955/B004"],"citable":false,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"global","source_local_id":"reader_a:retrospective_surprises_md:1","source_type":"v12_reader_walks_wide","support_id":"sup_e868aaf9eb05f89d6e43","text":"The later transition markers were the surprise. Creation in 95:4, *thumma* plus return in 95:5, the exception in 95:6, and *baʿdu* in 95:7 together activated fixed `ط و ر/B004` more strongly than the immediate topography did: the Mount retrospectively became an image of successive human states as well as a place. The exception also corrected a deterministic stage-reading by splitting the post-descent trajectory.","trust":"legacy_unbound"},{"anchor_evidence":[],"anchor_evidence_coverage":{"boundary":"Exact Arabic verifies surface contact only.
